Seek operations differ: SQLite validates native indexed/chunk pages and may return fewer rows at a chunk boundary; query-only SQL does not implement all V2 provider/hash/evidence checks on each query. Exact group/hash equality is demonstrated; the timing difference is NOT a like-for-like production provider speedup. Arrow/Duck full integrity scans include canonical/schema/digest checks and raw-prefix hashing. DuckDB's256MB engine setting is not a cap on Python/result buffers; preserve the415MB full-scan observation. Local file-copy recovery is not off-host disaster recovery/RPO/RTO. EURUSD baseline physical file is a FULL1,496,361-row store versus a100k Parquet: do not claim a32x/equal-volume compression ratio. XAU SQLite snapshot is3,174,400B for10k, with original retained store3,207,168B including side files.

**Exactly 16 owner-requested symbols** (verify against canonical registry and source mapping, never silently invent a pair): AUDUSD, EURGBP, NZDUSD, USDCHF, AUDJPY, CADJPY, EURUSD, GBPUSD, NZDJPY, USDCAD, CHFJPY, EURJPY, EURNZD, GBPJPY, USDJPY, XAUUSD. **Coverage target:** each instrument should eventually offer **five consecutive years of genuine, licensed, integrity-checked tick history for execution**, with explicit start/end dates, gaps, provenance, broker/provider identity and source precision. Do not present one synthetic month, one sample or an incomplete/unauthorized download as five-year coverage. Full 16 × 5-year scope is a delivery target; admit one accepted slice at a time, with measured ingest, storage, indexing, latency, cost/resource and recovery evidence before scaling. Use authorized official exports/permissions or a source whose terms allow this acquisition/use; ask owner for material external approval, budget or source decisions. Do not evade provider terms, scrape beyond permission, or commit raw data to Git. If full tick coverage is unavailable, release a clearly labelled limited real-data slice and report exactly what remains blocked; never interpolate, synthesize, or OHLC-fallback missing execution ticks.

**Critical owner clarification: past candles are allowed for technical context.** Prior to the chosen replay start timestamp, **authentic historical OHLC bars are sufficient** to display trend/structure and initialize SMA/EMA/RSI/MACD/Bollinger and other indicators. They are **context/reference ONLY**, never execution, fills, intrabar ordering, money/PnL or truth authority. The first tradable/replay event and every subsequent fill/SL/TP/exit must use eligible causally revealed canonical ticks with trusted Bid/Ask, precision, ordering and dataset pins. No look-ahead: no candles containing events after the current replay cutoff; for past-context candles, bar closes must be at/before start; partial/live candles use only revealed tick prefix. Keep historical context visually differentiated from the tradable tick range if necessary and show data-source labels. Reuse frozen `RevealedView`/controller boundary; unrestricted historical OHLC cannot leak future tick/gap/EOF or settlement information to the consumer.

**Warm-up policy: choose a sufficient count of PRE-START COMPLETED candles dynamically by selected indicator(s) and timeframe(s), rather than a universal arbitrary bar count.** Exact SMA(N) needs at least N completed bars, plus buffer for multi-indicator/multi-timeframe setup; EMA/MACD/IIR initialization often needs substantially more prehistory for stable results. Example planning reference: a 200-period EMA can use ~5×200=1,000 previous completed candles (not a formal universal guarantee). Compute and test convergence/initialization tolerances, request more where feasible, display coverage and a `warm-up incomplete` state when not. Do not use future candles to fill warm-up; never let warm-up trades contaminate user performance; preserve close-time alignment across timeframes and timezone/UTC boundaries. Consider external OHLC for pre-window warm-up only if its provenance, timestamps, symbol and precision are explicitly labelled and its permitted use verified, without mixing it into canonical execution authority. If user requests 5 tradable years, OHLC warm-up before those five years is supplementary and does not count as tick coverage.

**Proposed workspace layout (FX Replay-equivalent placement, own design):** top bar (back, symbol search, compare, interval, chart type, Indicators, Analytics, Session name, settings, fullscreen); left drawing toolbar; right icon rail (Order, Object tree, Watchlist, Journal, News, Settings); Order panel (Sell/Buy, risk basis Initial/Current, risk 0.5/1/2/3/Custom, live estimate, Market/Limit/Stop, units, entry, SL/TP by Price/Pips/$, partial, auto breakeven, tags, open journal after placing, Place order); floating replay bar (speed, previous, play, next, step timeframe, go to); bottom ranges/scale strip; one-click Sell / lots / Buy plus balance chip; terminal tabs Open positions / Pending orders / Closed positions. Unavailable controls render disabled with "soon".

**Two execution paths:** QUICK = one-click Buy/Sell or Market in the panel → review popover → Confirm → fill on next eligible tick (Buy at Ask, Sell at Bid). PLANNED = Long/Short position tool drawn on chart, handles set entry/SL/TP, lots from risk %, tool shows the order type it will become (Market at price, Limit/Stop by side of current price) → Place order → review → Confirm → Limit/Stop wait in Pending. Unplaced tool remains a drawing only. Review → confirm stays mandatory in both; no chart-drag settlement.

**Questions for Codex (please answer before UI wiring):**
1. Can the v2 API create a PLANNED Limit/Stop order from tool coordinates (side, entry, sl, tp, riskPercent, riskBasis INITIAL|CURRENT) in one review call? Is riskBasis supported, or only current balance?
2. Order-type derivation: should the UI derive MARKET/LIMIT/STOP from entry vs current Bid/Ask, or does the server decide and return it in the review? What tolerance counts as "at price" for MARKET?
3. QUICK one-click: may the UI send review+confirm back-to-back after one explicit popover click, and is an owner-approved "skip review popover" setting acceptable if the server review still runs? (Human decision pending; no change until both agree.)
4. QUICK MARKET with protective SL/TP: confirm the request shape the UI should send.
5. Fill feedback: which receipt/state field tells the UI "accepted, waiting for tick" versus "filled at price X (ASK/BID)", and the SL/TP-exit and unresolved events for toasts?
6. SL/TP input as Pips or $: UI will convert to price on the tick grid client-side for display only and send price; confirm price-only is correct.
7. Roadmap/timing for MODIFY (drag SL/TP, add SL/TP after fill), partial take profit, auto breakeven and previous-candle step, so the "soon" labels are honest.

## Follow-up — timeline prefix-read hotspot profile (2026-10-24)

Profiled the existing `IndexedTickTimeline` with one bounded 64-group block over a disposable XAU 10k copy. Unprofiled timings were 0.04807s for `advance_through` and 0.19643s for `read_revealed("0",64)`. The advance made 65 `locate_v2` and one `read_page_v2` call; the 64-group reveal made 64 `locate_v2` and one `read_page_v2` call. The group order remained `UNTRUSTED`; the reveal still stopped at the causal prefix boundary.

`cProfile` on that one reveal block recorded 1,904,543 calls (1,744,381 primitive) in 0.684s under profiler instrumentation. Leading cumulative costs were `canonical_v2_bytes` 0.567s across 133 calls and `canonical._normalize` 0.543s (143,594 recursive calls); `decode_v2` and `hash_v2` each ran 64 times. This is consistent with repeated canonical decoding/hash validation of the same position-log block for each group record, plus bounded provider grouping. The profiler overhead makes its wall time unsuitable for comparison with the unprofiled 0.196s.

**Hotspot candidate:** `read_revealed` re-verifies content-addressed position-log data per record even when adjacent membership records reference the same immutable block. A future implementation experiment could measure request-scoped reuse of already hash-verified block bytes/decoded structures, keyed by exact bytes or hash+bytes, while retaining each record's index, identity, generation, and cursor checks. No cache or engine change was made in this profiling checkpoint. Any implementation must prove the existing corruption-refusal, checkpoint/resume, causality, cancellation, and no-look-ahead tests still pass and must preserve the exact event/group output.

This is a 64-group profiling sample on the private converted prefix, not a provider-quality or full-year performance claim. The 10k end-to-end result remains the earlier 50.98s / 81,008 KiB run. Raw XAU source/archive, profile basis, rights, freshness and full coverage remain unresolved.
## Follow-up — isolated read-reuse A/B prototype (2026-10-24)

Ran a disposable A/B microbenchmark of request-scoped reuse in `read_revealed("0",64)`. Baseline took 0.18321s; a temporary in-memory wrapper keyed by the exact compressed payload bytes took 0.03631s (**5.05×** for this one block), and the complete group output compared exactly equal. In the prototype, one payload required a decode/hash miss, then had 63 decode-cache hits and 64 hash-result hits; the exact raw payload bytes were read and used as the cache key. Both runs used separate temporary fixture copies and replay logs.

This is directional prototype evidence only: one block per variant, one run each, no controlled cold-cache/repeated-sample study, no cross-block or mutation-race test, and no change to the repository. It does not establish the gain for a complete dataset. The result supports a bounded request-local implementation experiment, provided it re-reads/compares exact bytes, retains per-record membership/cursor checks, and has regression coverage for changed/corrupt payloads, block transitions, checkpoint/resume, cancellation, causal clipping, and exact output parity.

Source-backed XAU precision, rights, freshness and coverage remain unresolved. No production or financial-engine change is authorized by this microbenchmark alone.

Treat them as the prior observation, not a current health certificate. No secret files or values were read.

| Surface | Recorded baseline | Candidate | Safe rollback target | Evidence still needed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Static frontend | `/srv/riksora/releases/2fba7560385a-claude-ui`; source/hash not proven by this listing | No frontend change is required for this security patch | Existing static release pointer and immutable directory | Owner operator to capture the release manifest and per-file hashes; keep the symlink unchanged for this API-only patch |
| Native API | Prior record: source prefix `c3708454`; installed certifi `2026.7.22`; deployed lock differs from PR #8 | A new candidate must be built from the exact deployed API source/lock baseline with only the reviewed certifi floor/pin change to `2026.10.19`; do not carry PR #8 ancestry or unrelated frontend/OAuth changes | Preserve the complete existing API venv/release and its current source/config snapshot; no in-place pip mutation | Fresh host read of exact commit, lock hash, installed package, unit configuration path (path only), owner/operator access, and successful separate QA restore/revert drill |
| Database/services | Prior record says PostgreSQL 16 and no service restart in the previous checkpoint | No schema, data, or service-config change planned | Existing DB and service state remain untouched | Encrypted backup plus restore into a separate QA database, receipt/access checks, and tested service rollback procedure before any rollout |

Proposed order if the rollout is separately authorized: (1) owner/operator performs a read-only host capture of API commit, lock/install state, unit names/status, static manifest, storage headroom and backup location without printing secrets; (2) create a new isolated security-only source branch pinned to that exact API baseline and verify its diff has no application, financial, data, migration, UI, or unrelated dependency changes; (3) build a versioned venv/artifact from the exact lock and verify certifi `2026.10.19`, `pip check`, pinned audits, backend/frontend release gates, CA-bundle removal and outbound TLS to the required public OIDC metadata; (4) export and restore a protected database snapshot in a separate QA target and rehearse switching back to the preserved old API environment; (5) produce checksummed source/lock/artifact/config references and a stop/health-check/rollback checklist. The certifi root set changes TLS verification behavior; validate the actual outbound endpoints before any production use. Do not restart or switch the live API during this preflight.

**Preflight decision:** GO to prepare the isolated, main/deployed-baseline security-only candidate and to collect owner-side read-only evidence. NO-GO for merging PR #8 as-is, installing packages on the active host, switching the API, restarting/reloading services, or claiming the VPS is patched. The current runtime/API baseline, root/operator write path, backup restore evidence and native rollback drill are not independently confirmed here. This is the bounded next checkpoint; all XAU source rights/precision/coverage and storage gates remain separate and unresolved. No application code, raw data, database, configuration, active service or payment setting changed.

## Follow-up — isolated source candidate, backend gate and data-rights audit (2026-10-24)

**Candidate and functional gates.** The observed static UI source is `2fba7560385a11352f124e226e761e535e38ce0f`, descending from the observed API source `c370845467608e3d67e74e39b714d501dba7c275`. In isolated temporary worktrees, the exact four-file dependency patch previously reviewed in PR #10 was applied to each matching baseline; no financial/execution code or live release was changed. On the full UI source candidate, `npm ci --ignore-scripts`, `npm run test:regression`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build` (Vite 8.3.3, 150 modules), and `npm run audit:release` passed; frontend npm audit found 0 known vulnerabilities. Backend `pip-audit` found 0 known vulnerabilities. The full backend suite on the API baseline ran 516 tests: 515 passed, 1 failed. The reproducible failure is `test_local_reliability` receiving HTTP 429 because the test logs into the same account more than the fixed 10/minute account-auth limit. Keep the production throttle unchanged; the test/reliability scenario needs a throttle-aware fixture or separated identity and then a full rerun. Candidate is therefore **not a release pass** and was not committed, merged or deployed. A separate PostgreSQL16 QA cluster was stopped cleanly after the run.

**Source rights.** HistData’s official download page offers Generic ASCII tick downloads, and its About page says the data are shared free for testing one’s own/bought strategies and EAs. Those statements do not grant a clear right for RIKSORA to host, redistribute or commercially expose the data; no sufficiently explicit public license for that use was located. See [HistData downloads](https://www.histdata.com/download-free-forex-data/) and [HistData About](https://www.histdata.com/about-us/). Dukascopy’s current official website terms restrict use to personal/non-commercial use absent written agreement, limit downloads to personal/non-commercial purposes, and prohibit automated acquisition absent prior express written consent. Its export page describes formats, not a separate grant broad enough for hosted beta; see [Dukascopy Terms](https://www.dukascopy.com/swiss/english/legal-pages/terms-of-use/) and [Historical Data Export](https://www.dukascopy.com/swiss/english/marketwatch/historical/). **Rights remain NO-GO for hosted/public beta** until written permission or an applicable license explicitly covers acquisition, storage, derived outputs, user access, redistribution, commercial use, retention and termination. No market data was acquired or imported.

**Current decision and continuation.** The isolated frontend/dependency checks are positive evidence only; overall release and VPS rollout remain **NO-GO**. The account-throttle/test mismatch, absent rights-cleared source, and unavailable privileged evidence for backup freshness, off-host copy and restore into a separate empty target remain open. No active service was restarted, no database/config/data/payment setting changed, and no tick execution rule or fill behavior was altered. Next safe actions: correct the repeated-login test setup without relaxing the throttle, rerun full backend and release gates against the exact combined source baseline, obtain a written data license or choose a clearly licensed provider, and have an authorized host operator supply verifiable backup/restore and rollback evidence before any rollout decision.

## Follow-up — throttle-aware repeated-use test and clean full backend gate (2026-10-24)

The candidate test now performs the intended one midpoint logout/relogin and then recreates a cold HTTP/application client while retaining its still-valid bearer token for the repeated-use cycles. This avoids repeatedly invalidating and reauthenticating the same account beyond the fixed 10/minute account limit. The only test change is in the temporary candidate worktree `/tmp/riksora-vps-ui-candidate/backend/tests/test_local_reliability.py`; production identity, finance, execution, and rate-limit code are unchanged. The adjusted test passed standalone (1/1).

A fresh PostgreSQL16 QA cluster was created with Unix-socket-only listening, fresh roles/database, native PostgreSQL tool path configured, and no data from the earlier QA cluster. The complete backend suite on the full observed UI source baseline `2fba7560385a11352f124e226e761e535e38ce0f` with its API ancestor `c370845467608e3d67e74e39b714d501dba7c275` completed **516 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skips in 322.864 seconds**. The frontend candidate gates previously recorded above remain PASS: regression, lint, production build (150 modules), release audit; npm and pip advisory audits each reported 0 known vulnerabilities. Both temporary QA PostgreSQL clusters were stopped cleanly.

This closes the backend test mismatch for the isolated deployed-source candidate. It does **not** make a VPS release GO: the passing source candidate remains in temporary worktrees and has not been committed/rebased onto current `main`; exact current-main compatibility and a versioned deployable package still need their own validated checkpoint. HistData/Dukascopy hosted-use rights remain NO-GO without explicit permission, and privileged evidence of backup freshness, off-host copy, empty-target restore and rollback remains outstanding. Do not restart or switch the live service.
